9. 46d3bb15f55718bb (dolly, information_extraction, seed 0)
Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: A. A. Arnaud, born 1843, was a Franc who put all his craft into studying bugs. His big wins fit in a short list: writing about bugs in a Parisian journal, calling for an official group that tracks all bug illustrations, and marking 27 unknown PyrJay bugs.

A. Arnaud's bug list, lying at Musc's Palais Vilain, now has many bugs.

e/E count: 0; truncated: False

24. 2722f22dc137a37e (dolly, closed_qa, seed 0)
Prompt: Based on this paragraph about religions of the world, what are the "big five" religions?

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam are always included in the list, being known as the "Big Five". Some scholars also include other world religions, such as Taoism, Jainism, Sikhism, Zoroastrianism, and the Baháʼí Faith, in the category. These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

Answer: Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam form this list of big world faiths. In common thinking, additional faiths occupy a distinct comparison.

e/E count: 0; truncated: False

27. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 0)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A form for showing how atoms join, what bonds hold atoms by, and how atoms sit in a bond, such as H2O or C6H12O6.

e/E count: 0; truncated: False

Prompt: How do you calculate a golfer's handicap?

Answer: A golfr's total must go from 72 clubs of club A.
For clubs B and so on, multiply 10 by clubs in a round.

Add up 10 clubs and half A clubs in all 36 rounds a golfr hits.
Dividing by 18 accounts for 36/18 = 2 clubs in a round, annually.

Find a golfr's most low total from any 5 rounds. Call it a cal.
Multiply by 28.8 and call that golfr's handicap.

59. 46d3bb15f55718bb (dolly, information_extraction, seed 1)
Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: Aubin had a passion for fossil arthropods, aiming to classify all known arthropod forms. His work also had practical applications, such as aiding in antiquity dating, finding gold from tiny fossil parts, and guiding how to trap and transport fossil arthropods.

Aubin was a limb anatomist who had a firm grasp on physics. For his fossils, Aubin had a supply chain of a fossil fossil stocks, fossil grants, and fossil art. His fossil groups had a foundation at first, but Ruth's finding of individual fossil parts was crucial to transforming fossil groups into fossil classification.

e/E count: 0; truncated: False

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam are always included in the list, being known as the "Big Five". Some scholars also include other world religions, such as Taoism, Jainism, Sikhism, Zoroastrianism, and the Baháʼí Faith, in the category. These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

77. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 1)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A task can ask for a tool, a tactic, or a finding that points out how atoms bond within a crucial assumption. A distinction is that a goal can ask for confirmation of a known status, or ask for a finding about an unknown task.

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: A. Aran Co., on his 12th of January 1843, at M. F. said to him, "Ach! You call it a tomb, but this is but a box for an almost fossil." So was Bayburt. I found this pair.

M. Aran is known for his work in arthropods, in particular his 1869 publication on Malach.it, and his 1872 account of 27 mainly Pyrinozn ivory-wing moths from his Hays. His most famous work is An Account of an Ivory-wing Bollworm Caught in an Ivory Bury.

Of all his work, his fossil was a standing scandal from his diary.

Prompt: Based on this paragraph about religions of the world, what are the "big five" religions?

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam are always included in the list, being known as the "Big Five". Some scholars also include other world religions, such as Taoism, Jainism, Sikhism, Zoroastrianism, and the Baháʼí Faith, in the category. These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

Answer: 1. Hinduism
2. Buddhism
3. Judaism
4. Christianity
5. Islam

Also known as "Big 5", additionally hold a broad following around 3 billion individuals globally.

127. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 2)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A compound's formula shows atoms, bonds, and links among atoms. Its spatial layout and bonds join LUMO and HOMO and form a bond. A firm conclusion, not just a formula, allows a plan or action.

e/E count: 0; truncated: False

159. 46d3bb15f55718bb (dolly, information_extraction, seed 3)
Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: A full moon plot.

A bug artisan from a small town. His work and drawings list all sorts of Saturn Bugs, Caddis Flora, and blind Alms.

177. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 3)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A distinct atomic grouping can form a distinct function, binding fulfilling basic atomic tasks, such as forming bonds or carrying a function. A formula, such as N2O or Na3PO4, can show part of a distinction: atoms in it, and how many of all sorts. A diagram, such as a bond diagram, can shift from such part to full atomic and spatial info.

209. 46d3bb15f55718bb (dolly, information_extraction, seed 4)
Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: Aubin was a historian of biology and a staunch ally of Madam Roy. His photograph is now on display in a hall in his old town.

In a study, Aubin counts how many groupings fit a broad habit by classifying all groups, bringing a split in his count, and comparing it with a split in Madam Roy's account.

Aubin's strong association with Roy supports why Roy's study is worth doing.

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam are always included in the list, being known as the "Big Five". Some scholars also include other world religions, such as Taoism, Jainism, Sikhism, Zoroastrianism, and the Baháʼí Faith, in the category. These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

227. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 4)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A diagram to show how atoms join in a compound can carry info about atomic positions, bonds, and branching. Functional groups also count if all atoms follow a quantitat's law, such as valancy location.

It can also show what a particular atom has in its bonding orbitals, if that data is known. A structural formula adds bonds and branching, and can stand for a physical plug-in diagram, such as a carbon bond or a diagram in which a hydroxyl group is shown only by an arrow showing that group's bond to its atom.

e/E count: 0; truncated: False

259. 46d3bb15f55718bb (dolly, information_extraction, seed 5)
Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: Aab Douay was a Parisian naturalist, writing in 1869 on aids to moth hunting. His following book cataloging 27 unknown moth forms also won a champion's cup for arthropod hunting in 1871.

Douay did much trial and drill, as had all of his company's sons, including cladists and chrysids.

No common myths or fictions of his go with his actual pursuits.

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam are always included in the list, being known as the "Big Five". These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

277. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 5)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A structural layout shows how atoms join within a compound. For compounds with a known formula, it can also show how atoms stand in comparison to suitably arbitrary positions, such as comparison to a familiar format.

309. 46d3bb15f55718bb (dolly, information_extraction, seed 6)
Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: Aubin Arona was a 19th and 20th C inautho, known for his pitiful work in arthropods and for his cataloguing of many arthropod forms in Paris.

His books had major contributions to our grasp of many loath-making groups, and his small bug royal was both an icon and a prompt for a vast analysis.

Prompt: Based on this paragraph about religions of the world, what are the "big five" religions?

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam are always included in the list, being known as the "Big Five". Some scholars also include other world religions, such as Taoism, Jainism, Sikhism, Zoroastrianism, and the Baháʼí Faith, in the category. These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

327. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 6)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A bond joins atoms within a compound: a carbon-human link allows a human to walk, ground hog bonds allow growth, and so forth. A bond joins atoms in a distinct position within a compound. In a fixbond, owing to additional bonds, atoms can shift positions; in an ion, a bond might switch from pointing towards a human to pointing back at a human.

359. 46d3bb15f55718bb (dolly, information_extraction, seed 7)
Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: Ahab-o Pathin was a naturalist at work on food bugs and rock bugs of Pyran hid. His draw was a study of vast fauna, including 27 crinkly bugs prior to his 1870 books. Many bug sorts at Old Mus ont Pusan can still point.

e/E count: 0; truncated: False

374. 2722f22dc137a37e (dolly, closed_qa, seed 7)
Prompt: Based on this paragraph about religions of the world, what are the "big five" religions?

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam are always included in the list, being known as the "Big Five". Some scholars also include other world religions, such as Taoism, Jainism, Sikhism, Zoroastrianism, and the Baháʼí Faith, in the category. These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

Answer: Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam form what is known as "world's big 5" today.

377. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 7)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A physical plan or accounting notation shows how atoms and bonds form a group of atoms in a compound. It follows a formula. Both could call for a diagram. For an actual atom group, a diagram and a formula might go hand in hand.

402. 8d8cb5ac2300465e (dolly, open_qa, seed 8)
Prompt: How do you calculate a golfer's handicap?

Answer: Factors: gross
Gross=18 par*18=324.
Ball=s1, so 18*18=324 balls*1.
Total=s1+s2+s3+...s18.
Gross18-s18=disparity.
Disparity*18=s18*, so your handicap=324-s18*18.

Gain=s18*18-324.
Thus final handicap=324-s18*18.

Sorting your total into 18 ranks and adding up points is not part of this calculation.

409. 46d3bb15f55718bb (dolly, information_extraction, seed 8)
Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: Ablow's work falls into many paroxysms that could crush a calm mind. Augurs go wild: his proposal to study how furnishing locks could cut a sum of tolls is but an illustration. Cocoa is also a common accompanist to his study of ants.

Prompt: Based on this paragraph about religions of the world, what are the "big five" religions?

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam are always included in the list, being known as the "Big Five". Some scholars also include other world religions, such as Taoism, Jainism, Sikhism, Zoroastrianism, and the Baháʼí Faith, in the category. These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

Answer: Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity and Islam count as part of this list. Usually, folks with just that list call Buddhism and Hinduism as big part of world traditions, though TCM, Jainism, Zoroastrianism and Islam can also fit into this group.

427. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 8)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A drawing puts actual subunits in spatial form. It shows a carbonyl group and all bonds to which that atom adds. Atoms and bonds must match in count and form to a valid formula.

A formula, not just atomic symbols, adds critical info: a CO group AAdds a carbon to an O and a bond to both. A trial can show what would match a known formula. Such info is vital to atomic bonding and how compounds carry out a function.

Prompt: What is the scientific formula for table salt?

Answer: Salt is NaCl. It is a bond of a sodium atom and a chlor atom.

Mix Na with Cl in a 1:1 mass fraction to form NaCl. That fraction is a practical bound for salt in food.

It is not a formula with a count, such as C6H12O6 or CO2. It shows a valid bonding bond, not an actual count of individual atoms.

459. 46d3bb15f55718bb (dolly, information_extraction, seed 9)
Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: Aubin was a scholar in biology and a royal palacian in 19th-C. His famous works look at tiny inadorn or in a dark way and a bug catalog. Mauritius has a high count of a good-known kind of Chrysids.

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam are always included in the list, being known as the "Big Five". Some scholars also include other world religions, such as Taoism, Jainism, Sikhism, Zoroastrianism, and the Baháʼí Faith, in the category. These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

477. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 9)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A diagram showing all bonds and atoms in a compound shows its atomic bonding configuration, also known as its structural formula. A solitary bond links two atoms. A bond pair is a pair of atomic orbitals joining two atoms. A bond consists of two orbitals, which can form a particular bond with a matching atomic orbital on that atom. Atomic hydrids show how many bonds or unbound pairs form on a hydroxyl group. An atomic hybrid is a particular bond class that a primary atom has.

e/E count: 0; truncated: False

509. 46d3bb15f55718bb (dolly, information_extraction, seed 10)
Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: A small crustacian from a Pyr- an I and out of a fly pot. Back from his court in Lyon, his taps stand in a matching Old Loan as a moral subscription to his work.

Prompt: Based on this paragraph about religions of the world, what are the "big five" religions?

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam are always included in the list, being known as the "Big Five". Some scholars also include other world religions, such as Taoism, Jainism, Sikhism, Zoroastrianism, and the Baháʼí Faith, in the category. These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

Answer: Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam form a list of major world faiths. If additional schools qualify, such as with Jainism, Zoroastrianism or Sikhism, a tidy list might also call for that addition.

527. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 10)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A physical form combining atoms through bonds: that is a structural stipulation. A diagram or diagram (a standard form) also can stand for it.

Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: A.
A. was a natural historian who did important math in Cassonouth. A substantial fauna was a focus of his work, including a warm passion for a fauna off that Cassonouth. His actual patois would not allow him to sound as fancy as that.

A firm habit was to publish books about it. His work could fill a physical room, and its words could form an account. His thinking was about inquiry, not only about how to form accounts.

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam are always included in the list, being known as the "Big Five". Some scholars also include other world religions, such as Taoism, Jainism, Sikhism, Zoroastrianism, and the Baháʼí Faith, in the category. These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

577. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 11)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A formula showing bonds and all atoms within a chain runs through a signal. It is also a way of working out a compound's form and bonds. A compound's atoms and how far apart and which bonds span any slot adds up to bonds and point-form positions. In conclusion, it is how a 3D form and bonds can show up as a 2D formula.

609. 46d3bb15f55718bb (dolly, information_extraction, seed 12)
Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: Aubin was a jurisprudant born in Istria, Croatia, who is known as an author of books in Catalan, Ligurian, and Occitan. His works focus on placing plain pastoralism in bodily and social function. A principal work is Futuros, a book with historical insights into how rural living could work at a practical practical cost.

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

627. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 12)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

659. 46d3bb15f55718bb (dolly, information_extraction, seed 13)
Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: Aubry, a curator at Musuum national d'history, was a kind of oak for a thought. His study was on organisms from dark additions: round minnows, scorpions, and odd scar ab. His works had 27 of his own animal additions, as with an oak for a thought.

For his work on that fauna, a mosaic of fossil and living organisms, Sabayard could build a big pool of fossils. For a kind of scar, a box in an oak from 1879 now stands as proof that a small fly had a good look at a fray.

Prompt: Based on this paragraph about religions of the world, what are the "big five" religions?

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam are always included in the list, being known as the "Big Five". Some scholars also include other world religions, such as Taoism, Jainism, Sikhism, Zoroastrianism, and the Baháʼí Faith, in the category. These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

Answer: Hinduism, Buddhism, Islam, Christianity, and Judaism form a standard list of big 5 world faiths. This is usually a basic distinction, not a list of all world faiths in its strict form.

677. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 13)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A structural formula shows what atoms join and how far apart a pair of Hydro atoms must stand in a diatomic Hydro gas favor. It also lists all bonds, or pairs of Hydro atoms that touch. An illustration of Hydro, adding 2 Hydro atoms with linking bonds, is H-H. Atoms in Hydro go around in a planar triangular joint form. That and all positions Hydro occupy jointly form a 60 by within that unit of Hydro.

709. 46d3bb15f55718bb (dolly, information_extraction, seed 14)
Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: Aubin was a taxonomist who did work on Caddisfly fossils and in many Pyr. His books also catalog 27 unlook-at forms of armpit flat bark. A body of work is in Mus'um national d'hist.

e/E count: 0; truncated: False

Prompt: Based on this paragraph about religions of the world, what are the "big five" religions?

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam are always included in the list, being known as the "Big Five". Some scholars also include other world religions, such as Taoism, Jainism, Sikhism, Zoroastrianism, and the Baháʼí Faith, in the category. These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

Answer: Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam stand out in our world's spiritual tradition. Scholars call this grouping of major global faiths as "Hindu Buddhism Judaism Christianity Islam."

727. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 14)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A motif shows atoms and bonds within a compound. It says how many bonds hold atoms apart and what spatial positions atoms occupy. A minut
.

759. 46d3bb15f55718bb (dolly, information_extraction, seed 15)
Prompt: What is Elzéar Abeille de Perrin known for?

Elzéar Emmanuel Arène Abeille de Perrin (3 January 1843, Marseille – 9 October 1910, Marseille) was a French entomologist.

Abeille de Perrin was a lawyer in Marseille. He gave all his free time to entomology and was a member of the Société entomologique de France for twenty years. He was especially interested in the cave species of the Pyrenees. His best known publications are Monographie des malachites (1869), Études sur les coléoptères cavernicoles, suivies de la description de 27 coléoptères nouveaux français (1872), Notes sur les leptodirites (1878), and Synopsis critique et synonymique des chrysides de France (1878).

His collection of Palearctic, Coleoptera, Hymenoptera, Diptera, and Orthoptera is conserved in Muséum national d'histoire naturelle in Paris.

Answer: Aubin AB Anyway (Aubin, AB) was a famous Pyrind, with a vast folk hand. His work on Pyrind and his book on that biology got lots of study. His fossils, plus a book from 1878 and a small map, also show how much AQ is into this.

Aubin was also a famous biologist. His book on Malachits was striking. His map marks how many kinds AQ could find thoroughly. His Pyrind work is still part of what casual folk know about fauna.

World religions is a category used in the study of religion to demarcate the five—and in some cases more—largest and most internationally widespread religious movements. Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam are always included in the list, being known as the "Big Five". Some scholars also include other world religions, such as Taoism, Jainism, Sikhism, Zoroastrianism, and the Baháʼí Faith, in the category. These are often juxtaposed against other categories, such as indigenous religions and new religious movements, which are also used by scholars in this field of research.

Answer: Hinduism, Buddhism, Judaism, Christianity, and Islam form a commonly known group, known as both "Big 5" and "World's 5". Many also add additional faiths, as scholars say.

777. e1a9d912147a2708 (dolly, closed_qa, seed 15)
Prompt: What is a chemical structure?

A chemical structure determination includes a chemist's specifying the molecular geometry and, when feasible and necessary, the electronic structure of the target molecule or other solid. Molecular geometry refers to the spatial arrangement of atoms in a molecule and the chemical bonds that hold the atoms together and can be represented using structural formulae and by molecular models; complete electronic structure descriptions include specifying the occupation of a molecule's molecular orbitals. Structure determination can be applied to a range of targets from very simple molecules (e.g., diatomic oxygen or nitrogen) to very complex ones (e.g., such as protein or DNA).

Answer: A formula naming all a compound's atoms, and how such atoms link into units, is a standard approach to a formula's distinction from its physical composition. This formula can contain bonds and indications of atom sorts, but omit a broad aim or aromatics. Adding such indications can turn it into a full notation for a bond's plan or orbitals. A physical analysis is distinct from that formula.
